Well, I don't really know exactly how often. It's just... whenever I feel really bad, I guess. I haven't really kept track of the days or anything. I just grab the journal when I'm sitting on my bed feeling awful. My bed is actually right next to the window, and since I sleep during the day, I had to put up these blackout curtains. No, I don't think so. It always feels... out of my hands, I guess. Once I start eating, it's like I keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically. Even while I'm sitting there thinking, 'Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,' I just don't actually stop until the food is gone or I'm too uncomfortable to keep going. I've been trying to get back into sketching lately. Mostly charcoal stuff, portraits and things. I used to do it all the time in high school, but then charcoal is just so messy, you know? It gets all over your hands and under your fingernails. I spend half my shift scrubbing floors and wiping down counters, so the last thing I want is to look like my hands are covered in soot. I actually found this special pumice soap that mechanics use? It smells exactly like oranges, which reminds me of this orchard my parents took me to when I was little. We picked so many oranges we ended up having to make marmalade, and I ruined my favorite yellow shirt because the jam got everywhere. But anyway, yeah, I don't ever really feel in control when I'm eating like that. I just can't stop once I start.

Not really, no. I don't own a scale or anything, but my work pants seem to fit about the same as they always do. They're these terrible, stiff navy blue cargo pants they make us wear. I got terrible food poisoning from this little food cart parked near one of the offices I clean. It's usually set up right next to this old laundromat with a broken neon sign. The sign flashes this bright pink light, and it's so annoying when I'm waiting for my bus at 3 AM. I actually tried calling the city to complain about the light once because it's basically a public nuisance, but they just put me on hold forever. They play that really generic jazz elevator music? I actually recognized the exact song from a true crime podcast I listen to while I mop. But, um... yeah, no. I don't take laxatives or make myself sick or anything like that. I just sit there feeling way too full and miserable.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Well, for the past several months, it's been happening... I'd say there's usually at least one night each week where I stop at the 24-hour grocery store after my shift. I buy way too much food and just eat it all in one stretch when I get home. I can't remember a recent month where it stopped for more than a week or so. It's always that same grocery store, too. The one on 4th Street? They have this cashier, Gary, who works the overnight shift, and he always wears these really loud, patterned ties. Like, last week he had one with little cartoon tacos all over it, which I thought was funny but also kind of weird for a grocery store uniform. I wonder where he even finds ties like that. Maybe he makes them? My grandma used to sew, she made me this beautiful quilt out of my old band t-shirts when I went off to college. I only stayed for three semesters before I had to drop out, but I still have the quilt. It's on my bed right now, actually. But yeah, to answer your question, it's about once a week that I do that whole eating thing.\n\nDoctor: How often do you write those mean things about yourself in your journal?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really know exactly how often. It's just... whenever I feel really bad, I guess. I haven't really kept track of the days or anything. I just grab the journal when I'm sitting on my bed feeling awful. My bed is actually right next to the window, and since I sleep during the day, I had to put up these blackout curtains. They're supposed to block out 100% of the light, but there's always this tiny crack at the bottom where the sun sneaks in. It drives me crazy. I tried taping it down with duct tape once, but it peeled the paint off the windowsill, and my landlord got so mad. He's this older guy who always smells like peppermint and cigars, which is such a weird combination. He actually threatened to keep my security deposit over it, which I really need if I'm ever going to move out of that place. The plumbing is terrible anyway. But, um... yeah, I don't really count how many times I write in the journal. Just when the feeling hits me, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It always feels... out of my hands, I guess. Once I start eating, it's like I keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically. Even while I'm sitting there thinking, 'Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,' I just don't actually stop until the food is gone or I'm too uncomfortable to keep going. I've been trying to get back into sketching lately. Mostly charcoal stuff, portraits and things. I used to do it all the time in high school, but then charcoal is just so messy, you know? It gets all over your hands and under your fingernails. I spend half my shift scrubbing floors and wiping down counters, so the last thing I want is to look like my hands are covered in soot. I actually found this special pumice soap that mechanics use? It smells exactly like oranges, which reminds me of this orchard my parents took me to when I was little. We picked so many oranges we ended up having to make marmalade, and I ruined my favorite yellow shirt because the jam got everywhere. But anyway, yeah, I don't ever really feel in control when I'm eating like that. I just can't stop once I start.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don't own a scale or anything, but my work pants seem to fit about the same as they always do. They're these terrible, stiff navy blue cargo pants they make us wear. I swear the material is completely indestructible, but it's so uncomfortable. I've washed them a million times trying to soften them up, but the washing machines in my apartment building are basically useless. They always smell like mildew. I usually end up dragging all my stuff to the laundromat two blocks over. Last time I was there, someone left a single red sock hidden in the dryer, and it turned all my white undershirts this weird dusty pink color. I tried soaking them in bleach, but honestly, I smell enough bleach at work. I spend half my shift scrubbing the bathrooms on the third floor of this office building, and the smell just clings to your hair. There's this one woman on that floor who leaves half-drank lattes on her desk every Friday, and by Monday they're practically a science experiment. I don't understand how people can just leave a mess like that for someone else to deal with. But anyway, yeah, to answer your question, my weight hasn't really changed that I've noticed.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah, I do. That's actually... that's kind of why I came in to see you. After it happens, I just feel so gross and disappointed in myself. It's like, I'm 24, I should have more control over myself, you know? And then I end up writing all those awful things about myself in my journal. There's this one that does Korean BBQ tacos, and the line is always wrapped around the block. I tried making my own at home once, but I couldn't get the marinade right. It calls for Asian pears, which are surprisingly hard to find at my regular grocery store. I usually have to take the bus all the way to the international market on the other side of town, and the buses here are just so unreliable. Last week, I waited forty-five minutes in the freezing rain because two buses in a row just drove right past my stop without even slowing down. I work nights as a janitor, mostly office buildings, which is okay because it's quiet. Sometimes I listen to audiobooks while I mop. But yeah, the journal... I just write these really mean things about myself after I eat like that. It's just a lot of self-hatred on paper.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I never do that. I actually really, really hate throwing up. I think the last time I was sick like that was... gosh, maybe two years ago? I got terrible food poisoning from this little food cart parked near one of the offices I clean. It's usually set up right next to this old laundromat with a broken neon sign. The sign flashes this bright pink light, and it's so annoying when I'm waiting for my bus at 3 AM. I actually tried calling the city to complain about the light once because it's basically a public nuisance, but they just put me on hold forever. They play that really generic jazz elevator music? I actually recognized the exact song from a true crime podcast I listen to while I mop. But, um... yeah, no. I don't take laxatives or make myself sick or anything like that. I just sit there feeling way too full and miserable.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Well, for the past several months, it's been happening... I'd say there's usually at least one night each week where I stop at the 24-hour grocery store after my shift. I buy way too much food and just eat it all in one stretch when I get home. I can't remember a recent month where it stopped for more than a week or so. It's always that same grocery store, too. The one on 4th Street? They have this cashier, Gary, who works the overnight shift, and he always wears these really loud, patterned ties. Like, last week he had one with little cartoon tacos all over it, which I thought was funny but also kind of weird for a grocery store uniform. I wonder where he even finds ties like that. Maybe he makes them? My grandma used to sew, she made me this beautiful quilt out of my old band t-shirts when I went off to college. I only stayed for three semesters before I had to drop out, but I still have the quilt. It's on my bed right now, actually. But yeah, to answer your question, it's about once a week that I do that whole eating thing.\n\nDoctor: How often do you write those mean things about yourself in your journal?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really know exactly how often. It's just... whenever I feel really bad, I guess. I haven't really kept track of the days or anything. I just grab the journal when I'm sitting on my bed feeling awful. My bed is actually right next to the window, and since I sleep during the day, I had to put up these blackout curtains. They're supposed to block out 100% of the light, but there's always this tiny crack at the bottom where the sun sneaks in. It drives me crazy. I tried taping it down with duct tape once, but it peeled the paint off the windowsill, and my landlord got so mad. He's this older guy who always smells like peppermint and cigars, which is such a weird combination. He actually threatened to keep my security deposit over it, which I really need if I'm ever going to move out of that place. The plumbing is terrible anyway. But, um... yeah, I don't really count how many times I write in the journal. Just when the feeling hits me, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It always feels... out of my hands, I guess. Once I start eating, it's like I keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically. Even while I'm sitting there thinking, 'Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,' I just don't actually stop until the food is gone or I'm too uncomfortable to keep going. I've been trying to get back into sketching lately. Mostly charcoal stuff, portraits and things. I used to do it all the time in high school, but then charcoal is just so messy, you know? It gets all over your hands and under your fingernails. I spend half my shift scrubbing floors and wiping down counters, so the last thing I want is to look like my hands are covered in soot. I actually found this special pumice soap that mechanics use? It smells exactly like oranges, which reminds me of this orchard my parents took me to when I was little. We picked so many oranges we ended up having to make marmalade, and I ruined my favorite yellow shirt because the jam got everywhere. But anyway, yeah, I don't ever really feel in control when I'm eating like that. I just can't stop once I start.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don't own a scale or anything, but my work pants seem to fit about the same as they always do. They're these terrible, stiff navy blue cargo pants they make us wear. I swear the material is completely indestructible, but it's so uncomfortable. I've washed them a million times trying to soften them up, but the washing machines in my apartment building are basically useless. They always smell like mildew. I usually end up dragging all my stuff to the laundromat two blocks over. Last time I was there, someone left a single red sock hidden in the dryer, and it turned all my white undershirts this weird dusty pink color. I tried soaking them in bleach, but honestly, I smell enough bleach at work. I spend half my shift scrubbing the bathrooms on the third floor of this office building, and the smell just clings to your hair. There's this one woman on that floor who leaves half-drank lattes on her desk every Friday, and by Monday they're practically a science experiment. I don't understand how people can just leave a mess like that for someone else to deal with. But anyway, yeah, to answer your question, my weight hasn't really changed that I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. That's actually... that's kind of why I came in to see you. After it happens, I just feel so gross and disappointed in myself. It's like, I'm 24, I should have more control over myself, you know? And then I end up writing all those awful things about myself in my journal. The journal is this leather-bound one my sister gave me for my birthday last year. She got it from this little artisan market downtown. They always have these incredible food trucks there. There's this one that does Korean BBQ tacos, and the line is always wrapped around the block. I tried making my own at home once, but I couldn't get the marinade right. It calls for Asian pears, which are surprisingly hard to find at my regular grocery store. I usually have to take the bus all the way to the international market on the other side of town, and the buses here are just so unreliable. Last week, I waited forty-five minutes in the freezing rain because two buses in a row just drove right past my stop without even slowing down. By the time I got to work, my shoes were completely soaked through, and trying to mop floors in squeaky, wet sneakers is the worst. But, um... yeah, I feel really guilty and just... bad about myself after I eat like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

{"candidates": ["F50.4", "F50.5"], "note": "", "is_final": false}

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I do. I eat really fast during those times. I'll just be standing at the kitchen counter, or sometimes I'm sitting on my bed with my sketchbook still open, and I'm just... I'm barely even tasting anything, honestly. It's like I'm just trying to get it down before I can think too much about what I'm doing. It reminds me of how fast I have to eat on my lunch breaks at work. They only give us thirty minutes, and by the time I get all my cleaning supplies locked up in the utility closet—which the lock always gets stuck on, by the way. I've told the building manager, Mr. Henderson, like five times that it needs WD-40, but he never listens. He's always too busy talking on his cell phone in the lobby. He has this really loud, booming voice, and you can hear him echoing off the marble floors from all the way down the hall. Anyway, by the time I get my stuff put away and wash my hands, I have like fifteen minutes left to actually eat. So I usually just inhale a sandwich or whatever I brought. I've been trying to bring healthier things, like those little pre-packaged salads? But the lettuce always gets kind of slimy by the end of the week. But yeah, when I have those episodes at home, it's definitely like that. Just really, really fast.

I have this journal, actually, it's leather-bound with these little embossed leaves on the cover. I found it at a thrift store near the building I clean. I work nights as a janitor, mostly office buildings, which is okay because it's quiet. Sometimes I listen to audiobooks while I mop. But yeah, the journal... I just write these really mean things about myself after I eat like that. It's just a lot of self-hatred on paper.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I never do that. I actually really, really hate throwing up. I think the last time I was sick like that was... gosh, maybe two years ago? I got terrible food poisoning from this little food cart parked near one of the offices I clean. It's usually set up right next to this old laundromat with a broken neon sign. The sign flashes this bright pink light, and it's so annoying when I'm waiting for my bus at 3 AM. I actually tried calling the city to complain about the light once because it's basically a public nuisance, but they just put me on hold forever. They play that really generic jazz elevator music? I actually recognized the exact song from a true crime podcast I listen to while I mop. But, um... yeah, no. I don't take laxatives or make myself sick or anything like that. I just sit there feeling way too full and miserable.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Well, for the past several months, it's been happening... I'd say there's usually at least one night each week where I stop at the 24-hour grocery store after my shift. I buy way too much food and just eat it all in one stretch when I get home. I can't remember a recent month where it stopped for more than a week or so. It's always that same grocery store, too. The one on 4th Street? They have this cashier, Gary, who works the overnight shift, and he always wears these really loud, patterned ties. Like, last week he had one with little cartoon tacos all over it, which I thought was funny but also kind of weird for a grocery store uniform. I wonder where he even finds ties like that. Maybe he makes them? My grandma used to sew, she made me this beautiful quilt out of my old band t-shirts when I went off to college. I only stayed for three semesters before I had to drop out, but I still have the quilt. It's on my bed right now, actually. But yeah, to answer your question, it's about once a week that I do that whole eating thing.\n\nDoctor: How often do you write those mean things about yourself in your journal?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really know exactly how often. It's just... whenever I feel really bad, I guess. I haven't really kept track of the days or anything. I just grab the journal when I'm sitting on my bed feeling awful. My bed is actually right next to the window, and since I sleep during the day, I had to put up these blackout curtains. They're supposed to block out 100% of the light, but there's always this tiny crack at the bottom where the sun sneaks in. It drives me crazy. I tried taping it down with duct tape once, but it peeled the paint off the windowsill, and my landlord got so mad. He's this older guy who always smells like peppermint and cigars, which is such a weird combination. He actually threatened to keep my security deposit over it, which I really need if I'm ever going to move out of that place. The plumbing is terrible anyway. But, um... yeah, I don't really count how many times I write in the journal. Just when the feeling hits me, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It always feels... out of my hands, I guess. Once I start eating, it's like I keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically. Even while I'm sitting there thinking, 'Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,' I just don't actually stop until the food is gone or I'm too uncomfortable to keep going. I've been trying to get back into sketching lately. Mostly charcoal stuff, portraits and things. I used to do it all the time in high school, but then charcoal is just so messy, you know? It gets all over your hands and under your fingernails. I spend half my shift scrubbing floors and wiping down counters, so the last thing I want is to look like my hands are covered in soot. I actually found this special pumice soap that mechanics use? It smells exactly like oranges, which reminds me of this orchard my parents took me to when I was little. We picked so many oranges we ended up having to make marmalade, and I ruined my favorite yellow shirt because the jam got everywhere. But anyway, yeah, I don't ever really feel in control when I'm eating like that. I just can't stop once I start.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don't own a scale or anything, but my work pants seem to fit about the same as they always do. They're these terrible, stiff navy blue cargo pants they make us wear. I swear the material is completely indestructible, but it's so uncomfortable. I've washed them a million times trying to soften them up, but the washing machines in my apartment building are basically useless. They always smell like mildew. I usually end up dragging all my stuff to the laundromat two blocks over. Last time I was there, someone left a single red sock hidden in the dryer, and it turned all my white undershirts this weird dusty pink color. I tried soaking them in bleach, but honestly, I smell enough bleach at work. I spend half my shift scrubbing the bathrooms on the third floor of this office building, and the smell just clings to your hair. There's this one woman on that floor who leaves half-drank lattes on her desk every Friday, and by Monday they're practically a science experiment. I don't understand how people can just leave a mess like that for someone else to deal with. And then I end up writing all those awful things about myself in my journal. The journal is this leather-bound one my sister gave me for my birthday last year. She got it from this little artisan market downtown. They always have these incredible food trucks there. There's this one that does Korean BBQ tacos, and the line is always wrapped around the block. I tried making my own at home once, but I couldn't get the marinade right. It calls for Asian pears, which are surprisingly hard to find at my regular grocery store. I usually have to take the bus all the way to the international market on the other side of town, and the buses here are just so unreliable. Last week, I waited forty-five minutes in the freezing rain because two buses in a row just drove right past my stop without even slowing down. By the time I got to work, my shoes were completely soaked through, and trying to mop floors in squeaky, wet sneakers is the worst. But yeah, when I have those episodes at home, it's definitely like that. Yeah, definitely. It's way more than a normal person would eat. Like, on some nights after my cleaning shift, I'll stop at the convenience store on the corner—the one with the flickering neon sign that drives me crazy. The owner, this guy named Sal, he never fixes it. He's always sitting behind the counter watching these really old black-and-white westerns on a tiny portable TV. I don't know how he can even see the screen, it's so small and grainy. But anyway, I'll stop there just meaning to grab dinner, and I end up buying a big bowl of ramen, several rice balls, some fried chicken, and a bunch of sweets. And then I take it all back to my room and eat the whole thing in like an hour or two. My room is so small, too, so the smell of the fried chicken just lingers for days. I tried lighting one of those expensive soy candles my mom got me for Christmas to cover it up. It's supposed to smell like "ocean breeze" or something, but it just smells like cheap soap. My mom is obsessed with candles. She has this whole closet in her house just dedicated to storing them, all organized by season. She gets really upset if you burn a winter candle in the summer. But um, yeah, it's a lot of food. Way more than I should be eating.

I actually recognized the exact song from a true crime podcast I listen to while I mop. But, um... yeah, no. I don't take laxatives or make myself sick or anything like that. I just sit there feeling way too full and miserable.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Well, for the past several months, it's been happening... I'd say there's usually at least one night each week where I stop at the 24-hour grocery store after my shift. I buy way too much food and just eat it all in one stretch when I get home. I can't remember a recent month where it stopped for more than a week or so. It's always that same grocery store, too. The one on 4th Street? They have this cashier, Gary, who works the overnight shift, and he always wears these really loud, patterned ties. Maybe he makes them? My grandma used to sew, she made me this beautiful quilt out of my old band t-shirts when I went off to college. I only stayed for three semesters before I had to drop out, but I still have the quilt. It's on my bed right now, actually. But yeah, to answer your question, it's about once a week that I do that whole eating thing.\n\nDoctor: How often do you write those mean things about yourself in your journal?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really know exactly how often. It's just... whenever I feel really bad, I guess. I haven't really kept track of the days or anything. I just grab the journal when I'm sitting on my bed feeling awful. He's this older guy who always smells like peppermint and cigars, which is such a weird combination. But, um... yeah, I don't really count how many times I write in the journal. Just when the feeling hits me, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Even while I'm sitting there thinking, 'Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,' I just don't actually stop until the food is gone or I'm too uncomfortable to keep going. I've been trying to get back into sketching lately. Mostly charcoal stuff, portraits and things. I used to do it all the time in high school, but then charcoal is just so messy, you know? It gets all over your hands and under your fingernails. I spend half my shift scrubbing floors and wiping down counters, so the last thing I want is to look like my hands are covered in soot. It's just... whenever I feel really bad, I guess. I haven't really kept track of the days or anything. I just grab the journal when I'm sitting on my bed feeling awful. My bed is actually right next to the window, and since I sleep during the day, I had to put up these blackout curtains. They're supposed to block out 100% of the light, but there's always this tiny crack at the bottom where the sun sneaks in. It drives me crazy. I tried taping it down with duct tape once, but it peeled the paint off the windowsill, and my landlord got so mad. He's this older guy who always smells like peppermint and cigars, which is such a weird combination. He actually threatened to keep my security deposit over it, which I really need if I'm ever going to move out of that place. The plumbing is terrible anyway. But, um... yeah, I don't really count how many times I write in the journal. Just when the feeling hits me, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It always feels... out of my hands, I guess. Once I start eating, it's like I keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically. Even while I'm sitting there thinking, 'Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,' I just don't actually stop until the food is gone or I'm too uncomfortable to keep going. I've been trying to get back into sketching lately. Mostly charcoal stuff, portraits and things. I used to do it all the time in high school, but then charcoal is just so messy, you know? It gets all over your hands and under your fingernails. I spend half my shift scrubbing floors and wiping down counters, so the last thing I want is to look like my hands are covered in soot. I actually found this special pumice soap that mechanics use? It smells exactly like oranges, which reminds me of this orchard my parents took me to when I was little. I swear the material is completely indestructible, but it's so uncomfortable. I usually end up dragging all my stuff to the laundromat two blocks over. Last time I was there, someone left a single red sock hidden in the dryer, and it turned all my white undershirts this weird dusty pink color. I tried soaking them in bleach, but honestly, I smell enough bleach at work. I spend half my shift scrubbing the bathrooms on the third floor of this office building, and the smell just clings to your hair. There's this one woman on that floor who leaves half-drank lattes on her desk every Friday, and by Monday they're practically a science experiment. I don't understand how people can just leave a mess like that for someone else to deal with. And then I end up writing all those awful things about myself in my journal. The journal is this leather-bound one my sister gave me for my birthday last year. She got it from this little artisan market downtown. They always have these incredible food trucks there. There's this one that does Korean BBQ tacos, and the line is always wrapped around the block. I tried making my own at home once, but I couldn't get the marinade right. It calls for Asian pears, which are surprisingly hard to find at my regular grocery store. I usually have to take the bus all the way to the international market on the other side of town, and the buses here are just so unreliable. Last week, I waited forty-five minutes in the freezing rain because two buses in a row just drove right past my stop without even slowing down. By the time I got to work, my shoes were completely soaked through, and trying to mop floors in squeaky, wet sneakers is the worst. But, um... yeah, I feel really guilty and just... bad about myself after I eat like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I eat really fast during those times. I'll just be standing at the kitchen counter, or sometimes I'm sitting on my bed with my sketchbook still open, and I'm just... I'm barely even tasting anything, honestly. It's like I'm just trying to get it down before I can think too much about what I'm doing. It reminds me of how fast I have to eat on my lunch breaks at work. They only give us thirty minutes, and by the time I get all my cleaning supplies locked up in the utility closet—which the lock always gets stuck on, by the way. I've told the building manager, Mr. Henderson, like five times that it needs WD-40, but he never listens. He's always too busy talking on his cell phone in the lobby. He has this really loud, booming voice, and you can hear him echoing off the marble floors from all the way down the hall. Anyway, by the time I get my stuff put away and wash my hands, I have like fifteen minutes left to actually eat. So I usually just inhale a sandwich or whatever I brought. Wait, didn't you just ask me that? Or... wait, did you ask it differently? I'm pretty sure I just said that, right? That I always feel terrible afterwards. I mean, I don't think I've ever *not* felt guilty. It's like this heavy feeling in my stomach, which is funny because my stomach is already full, but it's a different kind of heavy. Kind of like when I accidentally dropped my supervisor's master key ring down the elevator shaft last month. Oh my gosh, that was a nightmare. The keys made this horrible clanging sound all the way down, just echoing. We had to call the elevator maintenance guy, Frank. He's this older guy who always wears suspenders and smells like peppermint and stale coffee. He was so mad, grumbling the whole time he was fishing them out of the pit. He kept talking about how back in his day, people respected their tools. I tried to offer him one of my peppermint candies to smooth things over, but he just glared at me. I get those candies in bulk from this warehouse store my sister has a membership to. You can buy like a five-pound bag for ten dollars. But yeah, no, I've never had an episode without feeling guilty. I thought I just answered that, but maybe I got distracted. Sorry.

I usually end up dragging all my stuff to the laundromat two blocks over. Last time I was there, someone left a single red sock hidden in the dryer, and it turned all my white undershirts this weird dusty pink color. I tried soaking them in bleach, but honestly, I smell enough bleach at work. I spend half my shift scrubbing the bathrooms on the third floor of this office building, and the smell just clings to your hair. There's this one woman on that floor who leaves half-drank lattes on her desk every Friday, and by Monday they're practically a science experiment. I don't understand how people can just leave a mess like that for someone else to deal with. She got it from this little artisan market downtown. They always have these incredible food trucks there. There's this one that does Korean BBQ tacos, and the line is always wrapped around the block. I tried making my own at home once, but I couldn't get the marinade right. It calls for Asian pears, which are surprisingly hard to find at my regular grocery store. barely tasting anything and just trying to get it down before I can think too much.\n- key: S053\n  name: Eating_Without_Hunger\n  description: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n  manifestation: A lot of the time I’m not even hungry, like I might have already eaten after work, but if I’m tired or stuck on a design idea I still start opening snacks and keep going.\n- key: S055\n  name: Negative_Affect_After_Binge\n  description: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n  manifestation: I came in because after these eating episodes I feel so gross and disappointed in myself, like I should have more control, and I end up writing awful things about myself in my journal.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional It's way more than a normal person would eat. Like, on some nights after my cleaning shift, I'll stop at the convenience store on the corner—the one with the flickering neon sign that drives me crazy. The owner, this guy named Sal, he never fixes it. He's always sitting behind the counter watching these really old black-and-white westerns on a tiny portable TV. I don't know how he can even see the screen, it's so small and grainy. But anyway, I'll stop there just meaning to grab dinner, and I end up buying a big bowl of ramen, several rice balls, some fried chicken, and a bunch of sweets. And then I take it all back to my room and eat the whole thing in like an hour or two. My room is so small, too, so the smell of the fried chicken just lingers for days. I tried lighting one of those expensive soy candles my mom got me for Christmas to cover it up. It's supposed to smell like \"ocean breeze\" or something, but it just smells like cheap soap. She's always been the one with her life together, honestly. She just got promoted to regional manager at her company, and she gets to travel all over the place. Last month she went to Chicago and brought me back this deep-dish pizza. It was frozen, obviously, packed in dry ice, but it was still so good. The dry ice was actually really cool, I put a piece of it in a bowl of warm water in the sink and watched it fog up the whole kitchen. It looked like a haunted house or something. I love Halloween. It's the only time of year I actually like going out, because you can dress up and just be someone else for a night. Last year I went as a ghost, but like, a really fancy Victorian ghost. I found this amazing lace dress at a thrift store... but anyway, no, I've never had an episode where I didn't feel just completely terrible and guilty afterwards. It always ends with me feeling bad and writing in that journal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode of eating a large amount of food in a short time without feeling guilty or bad about yourself afterwards?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? It's like this heavy feeling in my stomach, which is funny because my stomach is already full, but it's a different kind of heavy. He's this older guy who always wears suspenders and smells like peppermint and stale coffee. He was so mad, grumbling the whole time he was fishing them out of the pit. He kept talking about how back in his day, people respected their tools. I tried to offer him one of my peppermint candies to smooth things over, but he just glared at me. I get those candies in bulk from this warehouse store my sister has a membership to. You can buy like a five-pound bag for ten dollars. But yeah, no, I've never had an episode without feeling guilty. Um, well... I mean, I still make it to my cleaning shifts, and I do these freelance design events sometimes, so I'm getting by. But the next day at work, I just feel so distracted and ashamed. I spend half my shift scrubbing the bathrooms on the third floor of this office building, and the smell just clings to your hair. There's this one woman on that floor who leaves half-drank lattes on her desk every Friday, and by Monday they're practically a science experiment. I don't understand how people can just leave a mess like that for someone else to deal with. But anyway, yeah, to answer your question, my weight hasn't really changed that I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. That's actually... that's kind of why I came in to see you. After it happens, I just feel so gross and disappointed in myself. It's like, I'm 24, I should have more control over myself, you know? And then I end up writing all those awful things about myself in my journal. The journal is this leather-bound one my sister gave me for my birthday last year. She got it from this little artisan market downtown. They always have these incredible food trucks there. There's this one that does Korean BBQ tacos, and the line is always wrapped around the block. I tried making my own at home once, but I couldn't get the marinade right. It calls for Asian pears, which are surprisingly hard to find at my regular grocery store. I usually have to take the bus all the way to the international market on the other side of town, and the buses here are just so unreliable. Last week, I waited forty-five minutes in the freezing rain because two buses in a row just drove right past my stop without even slowing down. By the time I got to work, my shoes were completely soaked through, and trying to mop floors in squeaky, wet sneakers is the worst. It's way more than a normal person would eat. Like, on some nights after my cleaning shift, I'll stop at the convenience store on the corner—the one with the flickering neon sign that drives me crazy. The owner, this guy named Sal, he never fixes it. He's always sitting behind the counter watching these really old black-and-white westerns on a tiny portable TV. I don't know how he can even see the screen, it's so small and grainy. And then I take it all back to my room and eat the whole thing in like an hour or two. My room is so small, too, so the smell of the fried chicken just lingers for days. I can’t remember a recent month where it stopped for more than a week or so.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, definitely more than three months. For the past several months, it's been like... well, usually at least one night every single week where I get off work, go buy way too much food, and just eat it all in one stretch. I usually stop at this 24-hour grocery store on my way home from the office building I clean. It's so unbelievably bright in there at 2 AM, the fluorescent lights always give me a bit of a headache. They have this one night cashier, I think his name is Toby? He always wears these really loud Hawaiian shirts, even when it's freezing outside. I asked him about it once, and he said he collects them. He has like, over fifty of them. I can't imagine having that many shirts, honestly, my closet is so tiny. I live in this older apartment building and the closets are basically just glorified cupboards. I actually had to buy one of those rolling clothing racks just to hang up my jackets. The wheels on it are kind of squeaky, though, so I try not to move it around too much so I don't bother the neighbors below me. But anyway, um... right, the timeline. Yeah, I can't really remember a recent month where it stopped for more than a week or so. It's just been a regular thing every week for a while now.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because... I don't know, after these eating episodes I just feel so gross and disappointed in myself. Like, I should have more control, you know? It gets so bad that I end up writing awful things about myself in my journal. I have this journal, actually, it's leather-bound with these little embossed leaves on the cover. I found it at a thrift store near the building I clean. I work nights as a janitor, mostly office buildings, which is okay because it's quiet. Sometimes I listen to audiobooks while I mop. But yeah, the journal... I just write these really mean things about myself after I eat like that. It's just a lot of self-hatred on paper.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I never do that. I actually really, really hate throwing up. I think the last time I was sick like that was... gosh, maybe two years ago? I got terrible food poisoning from this little food cart parked near one of the offices I clean. It's usually set up right next to this old laundromat with a broken neon sign. The sign flashes this bright pink light, and it's so annoying when I'm waiting for my bus at 3 AM. I actually tried calling the city to complain about the light once because it's basically a public nuisance, but they just put me on hold forever. They play that really generic jazz elevator music? I actually recognized the exact song from a true crime podcast I listen to while I mop. But, um... yeah, no. I don't take laxatives or make myself sick or anything like that. I just sit there feeling way too full and miserable.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Well, for the past several months, it's been happening... I'd say there's usually at least one night each week where I stop at the 24-hour grocery store after my shift. I buy way too much food and just eat it all in one stretch when I get home. They have this cashier, Gary, who works the overnight shift, and he always wears these really loud, patterned ties. Maybe he makes them? My grandma used to sew, she made me this beautiful quilt out of my old band t-shirts when I went off to college. I only stayed for three semesters before I had to drop out, but I still have the quilt. It's on my bed right now, actually. But yeah, to answer your question, it's about once a week that I do that whole eating thing.\n\nDoctor: How often do you write those mean things about yourself in your journal?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really know exactly how often. It's just... whenever I feel really bad, I guess. I haven't really kept track of the days or anything. I just grab the journal when I'm sitting on my bed feeling awful. My bed is actually right next to the window, and since I sleep during the day, I had to put up these blackout curtains. They're supposed to block out 100% of the light, but there's always this tiny crack at the bottom where the sun sneaks in. It drives me crazy. I tried taping it down with duct tape once, but it peeled the paint off the windowsill, and my landlord got so mad. He's this older guy who always smells like peppermint and cigars, which is such a weird combination. He actually threatened to keep my security deposit over it, which I really need if I'm ever going to move out of that place. The plumbing is terrible anyway. But, um... yeah, I don't really count how many times I write in the journal. Just when the feeling hits me, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It always feels... out of my hands, I guess. Once I start eating, it's like I keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically. Even while I'm sitting there thinking, 'Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,' I just don't actually stop until the food is gone or I'm too uncomfortable to keep going. I've been trying to get back into sketching lately. Mostly charcoal stuff, portraits and things. I used to do it all the time in high school, but then charcoal is just so messy, you know? It gets all over your hands and under your fingernails. I spend half my shift scrubbing floors and wiping down counters, so the last thing I want is to look like my hands are covered in soot. I actually found this special pumice soap that mechanics use? It smells exactly like oranges, which reminds me of this orchard my parents took me to when I was little. We picked so many oranges we ended up having to make marmalade, and I ruined my favorite yellow shirt because the jam got everywhere. But anyway, yeah, I don't ever really feel in control when I'm eating like that. I just can't stop once I start.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don't own a scale or anything, but my work pants seem to fit about the same as they always do. They're these terrible, stiff navy blue cargo pants they make us wear. I swear the material is completely indestructible, but it's so uncomfortable. I've washed them a million times trying to soften them up, but the washing machines in my apartment building are basically useless. They always smell like mildew. I usually end up dragging all my stuff to the laundromat two blocks over. Last time I was there, someone left a single red sock hidden in the dryer, and it turned all my white undershirts this weird dusty pink color. I tried soaking them in bleach, but honestly, I smell enough bleach at work. I spend half my shift scrubbing the bathrooms on the third floor of this office building, and the smell just clings to your hair. There's this one woman on that floor who leaves half-drank lattes on her desk every Friday, and by Monday they're practically a science experiment. I don't understand how people can just leave a mess like that for someone else to deal with. But anyway, yeah, to answer your question, my weight hasn't really changed that I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. That's actually... that's kind of why I came in to see you. After it happens, I just feel so gross and disappointed in myself. It's like, I'm 24, I should have more control over myself, you know? And then I end up writing all those awful things about myself in my journal. The journal is this leather-bound one my sister gave me for my birthday last year. She got it from this little artisan market downtown. They always have these incredible food trucks there. There's this one that does Korean BBQ tacos, and the line is always wrapped around the block. I tried making my own at home once, but I couldn't get the marinade right. It calls for Asian pears, which are surprisingly hard to find at my regular grocery store. I usually have to take the bus all the way to the international market on the other side of town, and the buses here are just so unreliable. Last week, I waited forty-five minutes in the freezing rain because two buses in a row just drove right past my stop without even slowing down. By the time I got to work, my shoes were completely soaked through, and trying to mop floors in squeaky, wet sneakers is the worst. But, um... yeah, I feel really guilty and just... bad about myself after I eat like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I eat really fast during those times. I'll just be standing at the kitchen counter, or sometimes I'm sitting on my bed with my sketchbook still open, and I'm just... I'm barely even tasting anything, honestly. It's like I'm just trying to get it down before I can think too much about what I'm doing. It reminds me of how fast I have to eat on my lunch breaks at work. They only give us thirty minutes, and by the time I get all my cleaning supplies locked up in the utility closet—which the lock always gets stuck on, by the way. I've told the building manager, Mr. Henderson, like five times that it needs WD-40, but he never listens. He's always too busy talking on his cell phone in the lobby. He has this really loud, booming voice, and you can hear him echoing off the marble floors from all the way down the hall. Anyway, by the time I get my stuff put away and wash my hands, I have like fifteen minutes left to actually eat. So I usually just inhale a sandwich or whatever I brought. I've been trying to bring healthier things, like those little pre-packaged salads? But the lettuce always gets kind of slimy by the end of the week. But yeah, when I have those episodes at home, it's definitely like that. Just really, really fast.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the amount of food you eat during these episodes is more than what most people would eat in the same amount of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's way more than a normal person would eat. Like, on some nights after my cleaning shift, I'll stop at the convenience store on the corner—the one with the flickering neon sign that drives me crazy. The owner, this guy named Sal, he never fixes it. He's always sitting behind the counter watching these really old black-and-white westerns on a tiny portable TV. I don't know how he can even see the screen, it's so small and grainy. But anyway, I'll stop there just meaning to grab dinner, and I end up buying a big bowl of ramen, several rice balls, some fried chicken, and a bunch of sweets. And then I take it all back to my room and eat the whole thing in like an hour or two. My room is so small, too, so the smell of the fried chicken just lingers for days. I tried lighting one of those expensive soy candles my mom got me for Christmas to cover it up. It's supposed to smell like \"ocean breeze\" or something, but it just smells like cheap soap. My mom is obsessed with candles. She has this whole closet in her house just dedicated to storing them, all organized by season. She gets really upset if you burn a winter candle in the summer. But um, yeah, it's a lot of food. Way more than I should be eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've ever had one of these eating episodes without feeling guilty or bad about yourself afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, never. That's... I think I mentioned this earlier, but that's the whole reason I'm here. After it happens, every single time, I just feel so incredibly gross and disappointed in myself. It's like, I'm 24 years old, I should have more control over myself, you know? And then I end up writing all those awful things about myself in my journal. The leather one my sister got me. She's always been the one with her life together, honestly. She just got promoted to regional manager at her company, and she gets to travel all over the place. Last month she went to Chicago and brought me back this deep-dish pizza. It was frozen, obviously, packed in dry ice, but it was still so good. The dry ice was actually really cool, I put a piece of it in a bowl of warm water in the sink and watched it fog up the whole kitchen. It looked like a haunted house or something. I love Halloween. It's the only time of year I actually like going out, because you can dress up and just be someone else for a night. Last year I went as a ghost, but like, a really fancy Victorian ghost. I found this amazing lace dress at a thrift store... but anyway, no, I've never had an episode where I didn't feel just completely terrible and guilty afterwards. It always ends with me feeling bad and writing in that journal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode of eating a large amount of food in a short time without feeling guilty or bad about yourself afterwards?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? Or... wait, did you ask it differently? I'm pretty sure I just said that, right? That I always feel terrible afterwards. I mean, I don't think I've ever *not* felt guilty. It's like this heavy feeling in my stomach, which is funny because my stomach is already full, but it's a different kind of heavy. Kind of like when I accidentally dropped my supervisor's master key ring down the elevator shaft last month. Oh my gosh, that was a nightmare. The keys made this horrible clanging sound all the way down, just echoing. We had to call the elevator maintenance guy, Frank. He's this older guy who always wears suspenders and smells like peppermint and stale coffee. He was so mad, grumbling the whole time he was fishing them out of the pit. He kept talking about how back in his day, people respected their tools. I tried to offer him one of my peppermint candies to smooth things over, but he just glared at me. I get those candies in bulk from this warehouse store my sister has a membership to. You can buy like a five-pound bag for ten dollars. But yeah, no, I've never had an episode without feeling guilty. I thought I just answered that, but maybe I got distracted. Sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have these eating episodes and the feelings that follow them caused problems for you at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I still make it to my cleaning shifts, and I do these freelance design events sometimes, so I'm getting by. But the next day at work, I just feel so distracted and ashamed. It's like this cloud hanging over me. And sometimes I put off my sketching because I don't want to open my journal and look at the awful things I wrote about myself the night before. I use the same notebook for my sketches and my writing, which seemed like a good idea at the time because I like keeping everything in one place. It's this really nice leather one, like I said, and the paper is that thick, creamy kind that doesn't bleed through when you use ink. I love using these Japanese brush pens for my designs. They have such a fluid stroke. I actually ordered a new set online last week because my black one was drying out, but the delivery driver left the package on the wrong porch. He left it at apartment 4B instead of 4D. The lady in 4B is this older woman who has like, six cats, and the hallway always smells like pine litter. She's really sweet, though, she brought the package over and even gave me a slice of zucchini bread she made. I'm not a huge fan of zucchini in baked goods, it feels like it shouldn't be there, you know? Like carrots in cake. My sister makes an amazing carrot cake, though, with real cream cheese frosting. She uses this special vanilla extract she got in Mexico... but anyway, yeah, it definitely causes some problems because I just feel so out of it and avoid my drawing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having these eating episodes for more than three months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely more than three months. My mom is obsessed with candles. She has this whole closet in her house just dedicated to storing them, all organized by season. She gets really upset if you burn a winter candle in the summer. But um, yeah, it's a lot of food. Way more than I should be eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've ever had one of these eating episodes without feeling guilty or bad about yourself afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, never. That's... I think I mentioned this earlier, but that's the whole reason I'm here. After it happens, every single time, I just feel so incredibly gross and disappointed in myself. It's like, I'm 24 years old, I should have more control over myself, you know? And then I end up writing all those awful things about myself in my journal. The leather one my sister got me. She's always been the one with her life together, honestly. She just got promoted to regional manager at her company, and she gets to travel all over the place. Last month she went to Chicago and brought me back this deep-dish pizza. It was frozen, obviously, packed in dry ice, but it was still so good. The dry ice was actually really cool, I put a piece of it in a bowl of warm water in the sink and watched it fog up the whole kitchen. It looked like a haunted house or something. I love Halloween. It's the only time of year I actually like going out, because you can dress up and just be someone else for a night. Last year I went as a ghost, but like, a really fancy Victorian ghost. I found this amazing lace dress at a thrift store... but anyway, no, I've never had an episode where I didn't feel just completely terrible and guilty afterwards. It always ends with me feeling bad and writing in that journal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode of eating a large amount of food in a short time without feeling guilty or bad about yourself afterwards?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? Or... wait, did you ask it differently? I'm pretty sure I just said that, right? That I always feel terrible afterwards. I mean, I don't think I've ever *not* felt guilty. It's like this heavy feeling in my stomach, which is funny because my stomach is already full, but it's a different kind of heavy. Kind of like when I accidentally dropped my supervisor's master key ring down the elevator shaft last month. Oh my gosh, that was a nightmare. The keys made this horrible clanging sound all the way down, just echoing. We had to call the elevator maintenance guy, Frank. He's this older guy who always wears suspenders and smells like peppermint and stale coffee. He was so mad, grumbling the whole time he was fishing them out of the pit. He kept talking about how back in his day, people respected their tools. I tried to offer him one of my peppermint candies to smooth things over, but he just glared at me. I get those candies in bulk from this warehouse store my sister has a membership to. You can buy like a five-pound bag for ten dollars. But yeah, no, I've never had an episode without feeling guilty. I thought I just answered that, but maybe I got distracted. Sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have these eating episodes and the feelings that follow them caused problems for you at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I still make it to my cleaning shifts, and I do these freelance design events sometimes, so I'm getting by. But the next day at work, I just feel so distracted and ashamed. It's like this cloud hanging over me. And sometimes I put off my sketching because I don't want to open my journal and look at the awful things I wrote about myself the night before. I use the same notebook for my sketches and my writing, which seemed like a good idea at the time because I like keeping everything in one place. It's this really nice leather one, like I said, and the paper is that thick, creamy kind that doesn't bleed through when you use ink. I love using these Japanese brush pens for my designs. They have such a fluid stroke. I actually ordered a new set online last week because my black one was drying out, but the delivery driver left the package on the wrong porch. He left it at apartment 4B instead of 4D. The lady in 4B is this older woman who has like, six cats, and the hallway always smells like pine litter. She's really sweet, though, she brought the package over and even gave me a slice of zucchini bread she made. I'm not a huge fan of zucchini in baked goods, it feels like it shouldn't be there, you know? Like carrots in cake. My sister makes an amazing carrot cake, though, with real cream cheese frosting. She uses this special vanilla extract she got in Mexico... but anyway, yeah, it definitely causes some problems because I just feel so out of it and avoid my drawing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having these eating episodes for more than three months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely more than three months. Maybe he makes them? My grandma used to sew, she made me this beautiful quilt out of my old band t-shirts when I went off to college. It's just... whenever I feel really bad, I guess. I haven't really kept track of the days or anything. I just grab the journal when I'm sitting on my bed feeling awful. Even while I'm sitting there thinking, 'Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,' I just don't actually stop until the food is gone or I'm too uncomfortable to keep going. I've been trying to get back into sketching lately. Mostly charcoal stuff, portraits and things. I used to do it all the time in high school, but then charcoal is just so messy, you know? It gets all over your hands and under your fingernails. I spend half my shift scrubbing floors and wiping down counters, so the last thing I want is to look like my hands are covered in soot. I actually found this special pumice soap that mechanics use? It smells exactly like oranges, which reminds me of this orchard my parents took me to when I was little. We picked so many oranges we ended up having to make marmalade, and I ruined my favorite yellow shirt because the jam got everywhere. But anyway, yeah, I don't ever really feel in control when I'm eating like that. I tried soaking them in bleach, but honestly, I smell enough bleach at work. She got it from this little artisan market downtown. They always have these incredible food trucks there. There's this one that does Korean BBQ tacos, and the line is always wrapped around the block. I tried making my own at home once, but I couldn't get the marinade right. It calls for Asian pears, which are surprisingly hard to find at my regular grocery store. I usually have to take the bus all the way to the international market on the other side of town, and the buses here are just so unreliable. Last week, I waited forty-five minutes in the freezing rain because two buses in a row just drove right past my stop without even slowing down. By the time I got to work, my shoes were completely soaked through, and trying to mop floors in squeaky, wet sneakers is the worst. But, um... yeah, I feel really guilty and just... bad about myself after I eat like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I eat really fast during those times. I'll just be standing at the kitchen counter, or sometimes I'm sitting on my bed with my sketchbook still open, and I'm just... I'm barely even tasting anything, honestly. It's like I'm just trying to get it down before I can think too much about what I'm doing. It reminds me of how fast I have to eat on my lunch breaks at work. They only give us thirty minutes, and by the time I get all my cleaning supplies locked up in the utility closet—which the lock always gets stuck on, by the way. I've told the building manager, Mr. Henderson, like five times that it needs WD-40, but he never listens. He's always too busy talking on his cell phone in the lobby. He has this really loud, booming voice, and you can hear him echoing off the marble floors from all the way down the hall. Anyway, by the time I get my stuff put away and wash my hands, I have like fifteen minutes left to actually eat. So I usually just inhale a sandwich or whatever I brought. I've been trying to bring healthier things, like those little pre-packaged salads? But the lettuce always gets kind of slimy by the end of the week. But yeah, when I have those episodes at home, it's definitely like that. Just really, really fast.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the amount of food you eat during these episodes is more than what most people would eat in the same amount of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's way more than a normal person would eat. Like, on some nights after my cleaning shift, I'll stop at the convenience store on the corner—the one with the flickering neon sign that drives me crazy. The owner, this guy named Sal, he never fixes it. He's always sitting behind the counter watching these really old black-and-white westerns on a tiny portable TV. I don't know how he can even see the screen, it's so small and grainy. But anyway, I'll stop there just meaning to grab dinner, and I end up buying a big bowl of ramen, several rice balls, some fried chicken, and a bunch of sweets. And then I take it all back to my room and eat the whole thing in like an hour or two. My room is so small, too, so the smell of the fried chicken just lingers for days. I tried lighting one of those expensive soy candles my mom got me for Christmas to cover it up. It's supposed to smell like \"ocean breeze\" or something, but it just smells like cheap soap. My mom is obsessed with candles. She has this whole closet in her house just dedicated to storing them, all organized by season. She gets really upset if you burn a winter candle in the summer. But um, yeah, it's a lot of food. Way more than I should be eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've ever had one of these eating episodes without feeling guilty or bad about yourself afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, never. That's... I think I mentioned this earlier, but that's the whole reason I'm here. After it happens, every single time, I just feel so incredibly gross and disappointed in myself. It's like, I'm 24 years old, I should have more control over myself, you know? And then I end up writing all those awful things about myself in my journal. The leather one my sister got me. She's always been the one with her life together, honestly. She just got promoted to regional manager at her company, and she gets to travel all over the place. Last month she went to Chicago and brought me back this deep-dish pizza. It was frozen, obviously, packed in dry ice, but it was still so good. The dry ice was actually really cool, I put a piece of it in a bowl of warm water in the sink and watched it fog up the whole kitchen. It looked like a haunted house or something. I love Halloween. It's the only time of year I actually like going out, because you can dress up and just be someone else for a night. Last year I went as a ghost, but like, a really fancy Victorian ghost. I found this amazing lace dress at a thrift store... but anyway, no, I've never had an episode where I didn't feel just completely terrible and guilty afterwards. It always ends with me feeling bad and writing in that journal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode of eating a large amount of food in a short time without feeling guilty or bad about yourself afterwards?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? Or... wait, did you ask it differently? I'm pretty sure I just said that, right? That I always feel terrible afterwards. I mean, I don't think I've ever *not* felt guilty. It's like this heavy feeling in my stomach, which is funny because my stomach is already full, but it's a different kind of heavy. Kind of like when I accidentally dropped my supervisor's master key ring down the elevator shaft last month. Oh my gosh, that was a nightmare. The keys made this horrible clanging sound all the way down, just echoing. We had to call the elevator maintenance guy, Frank. He's this older guy who always wears suspenders and smells like peppermint and stale coffee. He was so mad, grumbling the whole time he was fishing them out of the pit. He kept talking about how back in his day, people respected their tools. I tried to offer him one of my peppermint candies to smooth things over, but he just glared at me. I get those candies in bulk from this warehouse store my sister has a membership to. You can buy like a five-pound bag for ten dollars. But yeah, no, I've never had an episode without feeling guilty. I thought I just answered that, but maybe I got distracted. Sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have these eating episodes and the feelings that follow them caused problems for you at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I still make it to my cleaning shifts, and I do these freelance design events sometimes, so I'm getting by. But the next day at work, I just feel so distracted and ashamed. It's like this cloud hanging over me. And sometimes I put off my sketching because I don't want to open my journal and look at the awful things I wrote about myself the night before. I use the same notebook for my sketches and my writing, which seemed like a good idea at the time because I like keeping everything in one place. It's this really nice leather one, like I said, and the paper is that thick, creamy kind that doesn't bleed through when you use ink. I love using these Japanese brush pens for my designs. They have such a fluid stroke. I actually ordered a new set online last week because my black one was drying out, but the delivery driver left the package on the wrong porch. He left it at apartment 4B instead of 4D. The lady in 4B is this older woman who has like, six cats, and the hallway always smells like pine litter. She's really sweet, though, she brought the package over and even gave me a slice of zucchini bread she made. I'm not a huge fan of zucchini in baked goods, it feels like it shouldn't be there, you know? Like carrots in cake. My sister makes an amazing carrot cake, though, with real cream cheese frosting. She uses this special vanilla extract she got in Mexico... but anyway, yeah, it definitely causes some problems because I just feel so out of it and avoid my drawing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having these eating episodes for more than three months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely more than three months. For the past several months, it's been like... well, usually at least one night every single week where I get off work, go buy way too much food, and just eat it all in one stretch. I usually stop at this 24-hour grocery store on my way home from the office building I clean. It's so unbelievably bright in there at 2 AM, the fluorescent lights always give me a bit of a headache. They have this one night cashier, I think his name is Toby? He always wears these really loud Hawaiian shirts, even when it's freezing outside. I asked him about it once, and he said he collects them. He has like, over fifty of them. I can't imagine having that many shirts, honestly, my closet is so tiny. I live in this older apartment building and the closets are basically just glorified cupboards. I actually had to buy one of those rolling clothing racks just to hang up my jackets. The wheels on it are kind of squeaky, though, so I try not to move it around too much so I don't bother the neighbors below me. But anyway, um... right, the timeline. Yeah, I can't really remember a recent month where it stopped for more than a week or so. It's just been a regular thing every week for a while now.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat during these episodes in secret, or do you eat with other people around?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I never eat with anyone else around when it happens. It's always just me, by myself. I make sure I'm alone. I usually wait until my roommate is asleep or out. She works these really weird hours at a call center, so sometimes she's gone all night. She handles customer service for some big electronics company, and she's always telling me these crazy stories about people yelling at her over broken toasters and stuff. I don't know how she does it. I'd cry if someone yelled at me on the phone like that. I actually hate talking on the phone anyway. Even making a doctor's appointment gives me anxiety, I always prefer to book things online if I can. But yeah, no, I definitely hide it. I wouldn't want anyone to see me like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Um, yeah, that's exactly how it feels. It's like... once I start, I just keep reaching for the next thing, almost automatically. Even while I'm sitting there thinking, "Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish," I don't actually stop. I just keep going until all the food is gone or I feel too sick to my stomach. It's like my hands are just moving on their own. I actually sprained my wrist a few years ago, not from eating, obviously, but from falling off my bike. I used to ride this vintage Schwinn cruiser everywhere. It was this beautiful seafoam green color. I found it at a garage sale for like, twenty bucks, which was such a steal because the tires were still in really good shape. The guy selling it was moving to Florida and just trying to get rid of everything. He had this whole collection of old vinyl records, too, mostly jazz stuff. I'm not really into jazz, it feels a little too chaotic for me. I prefer music with a really steady beat, like synth-pop. I've been listening to this one band from Sweden a lot lately, their lead singer has this amazing, ethereal voice. I tried to buy tickets to see them when they came to town last month, but they sold out in like, five minutes. The ticketing website crashed and everything, it was so frustrating. I ended up just staying home and watching a documentary about deep-sea creatures instead. Did you know there are jellyfish that can basically live forever? They just revert back to their polyp stage when they get old or injured. It's wild. But um, anyway, yeah, I just can't seem to stop myself once I start eating, no matter how much I tell myself to.
