========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, lose interest? I'm not really sure if I lose interest, honestly. I don't think I've ever really noticed that happening. I mean, usually when I'm eating like that, I'm just so focused on the food that I'm not really thinking about doing anything else anyway. It's not like I'm trying to draw or watch a movie at the same time. I tried watching a movie once while I was eating, it was that new sci-fi one with the time loops? My roommate insisted we watch it, but I couldn't really follow the plot. Time travel stuff always confuses me. Like, if you go back and change something, wouldn't that mean you never had a reason to go back in the first place? It just makes my head spin. I prefer movies that are more straightforward, like romantic comedies or documentaries. Oh, I watched this amazing documentary about competitive dog grooming last week. Have you seen it? They dye the poodles to look like pandas and tigers and stuff. It's so strange, but the dogs seem to really enjoy all the attention. I grew up with a golden retriever named Buster, and he hated baths. He would always try to hide under the porch whenever he saw the shampoo bottle. We had this big wrap-around porch on the house I grew up in, it was painted this really bright yellow color. My mom picked it out. She loved bright colors, she always wore these neon pink gardening gloves. She had this huge tomato garden in the backyard, and we'd eat fresh tomatoes all summer long. But, um... what were you asking? Oh, right, losing interest. Yeah, I don't think that's really a thing for me during those times. I just haven't noticed it.

I'd say there's usually at least one night each week where I stop at the 24-hour grocery store after my shift. I buy way too much food and just eat it all in one stretch when I get home. I can't remember a recent month where it stopped for more than a week or so. It's always that same grocery store, too. The one on 4th Street? They have this cashier, Gary, who works the overnight shift, and he always wears these really loud, patterned ties. Like, last week he had one with little cartoon tacos all over it, which I thought was funny but also kind of weird for a grocery store uniform. I wonder where he even finds ties like that. Maybe he makes them? My grandma used to sew, she made me this beautiful quilt out of my old band t-shirts when I went off to college. I only stayed for three semesters before I had to drop out, but I still have the quilt. It's on my bed right now, actually. But yeah, to answer your question, it's about once a week that I do that whole eating thing.\n\nDoctor: How often do you write those mean things about yourself in your journal?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really know exactly how often. It's just... whenever I feel really bad, I guess. I haven't really kept track of the days or anything. I just grab the journal when I'm sitting on my bed feeling awful. My bed is actually right next to the window, and since I sleep during the day, I had to put up these blackout curtains. They're supposed to block out 100% of the light, but there's always this tiny crack at the bottom where the sun sneaks in. It drives me crazy. I tried taping it down with duct tape once, but it peeled the paint off the windowsill, and my landlord got so mad. He's this older guy who always smells like peppermint and cigars, which is such a weird combination. He actually threatened to keep my security deposit over it, which I really need if I'm ever going to move out of that place. The plumbing is terrible anyway. But, um... yeah, I don't really count how many times I write in the journal. Just when the feeling hits me, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It always feels... out of my hands, I guess. Once I start eating, it's like I keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically. Even while I'm sitting there thinking, 'Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,' I just don't actually stop until the food is gone or I'm too uncomfortable to keep going. I've been trying to get back into sketching lately. Mostly charcoal stuff, portraits and things. I used to do it all the time in high school, but then charcoal is just so messy, you know? It gets all over your hands and under your fingernails. I spend half my shift scrubbing floors and wiping down counters, so the last thing I want is to look like my hands are covered in soot. I actually found this special pumice soap that mechanics use? It smells exactly like oranges, which reminds me of this orchard my parents took me to when I was little. We picked so many oranges we ended up having to make marmalade, and I ruined my favorite yellow shirt because the jam got everywhere. But anyway, yeah, I don't ever really feel in control when I'm eating like that. I just can't stop once I start.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don't own a scale or anything, but my work pants seem to fit about the same as they always do. They're these terrible, stiff navy blue cargo pants they make us wear. I swear the material is completely indestructible, but it's so uncomfortable. I've washed them a million times trying to soften them up, but the washing machines in my apartment building are basically useless. They always smell like mildew. I usually end up dragging all my stuff to the laundromat two blocks over. Last time I was there, someone left a single red sock hidden in the dryer, and it turned all my white undershirts this weird dusty pink color. I tried soaking them in bleach, but honestly, I smell enough bleach at work. I spend half my shift scrubbing the bathrooms on the third floor of this office building, and the smell just clings to your hair. There's this one woman on that floor who leaves half-drank lattes on her desk every Friday, and by Monday they're practically a science experiment. I don't understand how people can just leave a mess like that for someone else to deal with. But anyway, yeah, to answer your question, my weight hasn't really changed that I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. That's actually... that's kind of why I came in to see you. After it happens, I just feel so gross and disappointed in myself. It's like, I'm 24, I should have more control over myself, you know? And then I end up writing all those awful things about myself in my journal. The journal is this leather-bound one my sister gave me for my birthday last year. She got it from this little artisan market downtown. They always have these incredible food trucks there. There's this one that does Korean BBQ tacos, and the line is always wrapped around the block. I tried making my own at home once, but I couldn't get the marinade right. It calls for Asian pears, which are surprisingly hard to find at my regular grocery store. I usually have to take the bus all the way to the international market on the other side of town, and the buses here are just so unreliable. Last week, I waited forty-five minutes in the freezing rain because two buses in a row just drove right past my stop without even slowing down. By the time I got to work, my shoes were completely soaked through, and trying to mop floors in squeaky, wet sneakers is the worst. But, um... yeah, I feel really guilty and just... bad about myself after I eat like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I eat really fast during those times. I'll just be standing at the kitchen counter, or sometimes I'm sitting on my bed with my sketchbook still open, and I'm just... I'm barely even tasting anything, honestly. It's like I'm just trying to get it down before I can think too much about what I'm doing. It reminds me of how fast I have to eat on my lunch breaks at work. They only give us thirty minutes, and by the time I get all my cleaning supplies locked up in the utility closet—which the lock always gets stuck on, by the way. I've told the building manager, Mr. Henderson, like five times that it needs WD-40, but he never listens. He's always too busy talking on his cell phone in the lobby. He has this really loud, booming voice, and you can hear him echoing off the marble floors from all the way down the hall. Anyway, by the time I get my stuff put away and wash my hands, I have like fifteen minutes left to actually eat. So I usually just inhale a sandwich or whatever I brought. I've been trying to bring healthier things, like those little pre-packaged salads? But the lettuce always gets kind of slimy by the end of the week. But yeah, when I have those episodes at home, it's definitely like that. Just really, really fast.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the amount of food you eat during these episodes is more than what most people would eat in the same amount of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's way more than a normal person would eat. Like, on some nights after my cleaning shift, I'll stop at the convenience store on the corner—the one with the flickering neon sign that drives me crazy. The owner, this guy named Sal, he never fixes it. He's always sitting behind the counter watching these really old black-and-white westerns on a tiny portable TV. I don't know how he can even see the screen, it's so small and grainy. But anyway, I'll stop there just meaning to grab dinner, and I end up buying a big bowl of ramen, several rice balls, some fried chicken, and a bunch of sweets. And then I take it all back to my room and eat the whole thing in like an hour or two. My room is so small, too, so the smell of the fried chicken just lingers for days. I tried lighting one of those expensive soy candles my mom got me for Christmas to cover it up. It's supposed to smell like \"ocean breeze\" or something, but it just smells like cheap soap. My mom is obsessed with candles. She has this whole closet in her house just dedicated to storing them, all organized by season. She gets really upset if you burn a winter candle in the summer. But um, yeah, it's a lot of food. Way more than I should be eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've ever had one of these eating episodes without feeling guilty or bad about yourself afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, never. That's... I think I mentioned this earlier, but that's the whole reason I'm here. After it happens, every single time, I just feel so incredibly gross and disappointed in myself. It's like, I'm 24 years old, I should have more control over myself, you know? And then I end up writing all those awful things about myself in my journal. The leather one my sister got me. She's always been the one with her life together, honestly. She just got promoted to regional manager at her company, and she gets to travel all over the place. Last month she went to Chicago and brought me back this deep-dish pizza. It was frozen, obviously, packed in dry ice, but it was still so good. The dry ice was actually really cool, I put a piece of it in a bowl of warm water in the sink and watched it fog up the whole kitchen. It looked like a haunted house or something. I love Halloween. It's the only time of year I actually like going out, because you can dress up and just be someone else for a night. Last year I went as a ghost, but like, a really fancy Victorian ghost. I found this amazing lace dress at a thrift store... but anyway, no, I've never had an episode where I didn't feel just completely terrible and guilty afterwards. It always ends with me feeling bad and writing in that journal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode of eating a large amount of food in a short time without feeling guilty or bad about yourself afterwards?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? Or... wait, did you ask it differently? I'm pretty sure I just said that, right? That I always feel terrible afterwards. I mean, I don't think I've ever *not* felt guilty. It's like this heavy feeling in my stomach, which is funny because my stomach is already full, but it's a different kind of heavy. Kind of like when I accidentally dropped my supervisor's master key ring down the elevator shaft last month. Oh my gosh, that was a nightmare. The keys made this horrible clanging sound all the way down, just echoing. We had to call the elevator maintenance guy, Frank. He's this older guy who always wears suspenders and smells like peppermint and stale coffee. He was so mad, grumbling the whole time he was fishing them out of the pit. He kept talking about how back in his day, people respected their tools. I tried to offer him one of my peppermint candies to smooth things over, but he just glared at me. I get those candies in bulk from this warehouse store my sister has a membership to. You can buy like a five-pound bag for ten dollars. But yeah, no, I've never had an episode without feeling guilty. I thought I just answered that, but maybe I got distracted. Sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have these eating episodes and the feelings that follow them caused problems for you at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I still make it to my cleaning shifts, and I do these freelance design events sometimes, so I'm getting by. But the next day at work, I just feel so distracted and ashamed. It's like this cloud hanging over me. And sometimes I put off my sketching because I don't want to open my journal and look at the awful things I wrote about myself the night before. I use the same notebook for my sketches and my writing, which seemed like a good idea at the time because I like keeping everything in one place. It's this really nice leather one, like I said, and the paper is that thick, creamy kind that doesn't bleed through when you use ink. I love using these Japanese brush pens for my designs. They have such a fluid stroke. I actually ordered a new set online last week because my black one was drying out, but the delivery driver left the package on the wrong porch. He left it at apartment 4B instead of 4D. The lady in 4B is this older woman who has like, six cats, and the hallway always smells like pine litter. She's really sweet, though, she brought the package over and even gave me a slice of zucchini bread she made. I'm not a huge fan of zucchini in baked goods, it feels like it shouldn't be there, you know? Like carrots in cake. My sister makes an amazing carrot cake, though, with real cream cheese frosting. She uses this special vanilla extract she got in Mexico... but anyway, yeah, it definitely causes some problems because I just feel so out of it and avoid my drawing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having these eating episodes for more than three months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely more than three months. For the past several months, it's been like... well, usually at least one night every single week where I get off work, go buy way too much food, and just eat it all in one stretch. I usually stop at this 24-hour grocery store on my way home from the office building I clean. It's so unbelievably bright in there at 2 AM, the fluorescent lights always give me a bit of a headache. They have this one night cashier, I think his name is Toby? He always wears these really loud Hawaiian shirts, even when it's freezing outside. I asked him about it once, and he said he collects them. He has like, over fifty of them. I can't imagine having that many shirts, honestly, my closet is so tiny. I live in this older apartment building and the closets are basically just glorified cupboards. I actually had to buy one of those rolling clothing racks just to hang up my jackets. The wheels on it are kind of squeaky, though, so I try not to move it around too much so I don't bother the neighbors below me. But anyway, um... right, the timeline. Yeah, I can't really remember a recent month where it stopped for more than a week or so. It's just been a regular thing every week for a while now.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat during these episodes in secret, or do you eat with other people around?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I never eat with anyone else around when it happens. It's always just me, by myself. I make sure I'm alone. I usually wait until my roommate is asleep or out. She works these really weird hours at a call center, so sometimes she's gone all night. She handles customer service for some big electronics company, and she's always telling me these crazy stories about people yelling at her over broken toasters and stuff. I don't know how she does it. I'd cry if someone yelled at me on the phone like that. I actually hate talking on the phone anyway. Even making a doctor's appointment gives me anxiety, I always prefer to book things online if I can. But yeah, no, I definitely hide it. I wouldn't want anyone to see me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, that's exactly how it feels. It's like... once I start, I just keep reaching for the next thing, almost automatically. Even while I'm sitting there thinking, \"Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,\" I don't actually stop. I just keep going until all the food is gone or I feel too sick to my stomach. It's like my hands are just moving on their own. I actually sprained my wrist a few years ago, not from eating, obviously, but from falling off my bike. I used to ride this vintage Schwinn cruiser everywhere. It was this beautiful seafoam green color. I found it at a garage sale for like, twenty bucks, which was such a steal because the tires were still in really good shape. The guy selling it was moving to Florida and just trying to get rid of everything. He had this whole collection of old vinyl records, too, mostly jazz stuff. I'm not really into jazz, it feels a little too chaotic for me. I prefer music with a really steady beat, like synth-pop. I've been listening to this one band from Sweden a lot lately, their lead singer has this amazing, ethereal voice. I tried to buy tickets to see them when they came to town last month, but they sold out in like, five minutes. The ticketing website crashed and everything, it was so frustrating. I ended up just staying home and watching a documentary about deep-sea creatures instead. Did you know there are jellyfish that can basically live forever? They just revert back to their polyp stage when they get old or injured. It's wild. But um, anyway, yeah, I just can't seem to stop myself once I start eating, no matter how much I tell myself to.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you lose interest in things you normally enjoy during these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um, lose interest? I'm not really sure if I lose interest, honestly. I don't think I've ever really noticed that happening. I mean, usually when I'm eating like that, I'm just so focused on the food that I'm not really thinking about doing anything else anyway. It's not like I'm trying to draw or watch a movie at the same time. I tried watching a movie once while I was eating, it was that new sci-fi one with the time loops? My roommate insisted we watch it, but I couldn't really follow the plot. Time travel stuff always confuses me. Like, if you go back and change something, wouldn't that mean you never had a reason to go back in the first place? It just makes my head spin. I prefer movies that are more straightforward, like romantic comedies or documentaries. Oh, I watched this amazing documentary about competitive dog grooming last week. Have you seen it? They dye the poodles to look like pandas and tigers and stuff. It's so strange, but the dogs seem to really enjoy all the attention. I grew up with a golden retriever named Buster, and he hated baths. He would always try to hide under the porch whenever he saw the shampoo bottle. We had this big wrap-around porch on the house I grew up in, it was painted this really bright yellow color. My mom picked it out. She loved bright colors, she always wore these neon pink gardening gloves. She had this huge tomato garden in the backyard, and we'd eat fresh tomatoes all summer long. But, um... what were you asking? Oh, right, losing interest. Yeah, I don't think that's really a thing for me during those times. I just haven't noticed it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, yeah, regularly... I'd say it's been going on for about a year and two months now. It started right around the time I began trying to switch from my cleaning job into design, but I had to keep all my old janitorial shifts to pay the rent. It's just a lot, you know? Mopping floors at night and then trying to build a portfolio during the day. I actually work at this big corporate office building downtown, and the floors in the main lobby are this really porous travertine marble. It looks beautiful, but it's an absolute nightmare to keep clean. If someone spills coffee and you don't get to it in like five minutes, it stains. We have to use this special pH-neutral cleaner that smells like faint lemons. I actually don't mind the smell of lemons, it reminds me of this lemonade stand I had when I was a kid. My dad built the stand for me out of some scrap wood from a deck he was tearing down. He painted it white, but the paint started peeling almost immediately because he didn't use a primer. He was always rushing through projects like that. He tried to fix the plumbing under the kitchen sink once and ended up flooding the entire first floor. We had to live in a hotel for a week while they ripped up the hardwood. The hotel had a pool, though, which was pretty cool for a ten-year-old. I used to practice holding my breath underwater for as long as I could. I think my record was like, forty-five seconds. But um, yeah, so it's been about fourteen months since all this eating stuff started.

It's just... whenever I feel really bad, I guess. I haven't really kept track of the days or anything. I just grab the journal when I'm sitting on my bed feeling awful. My bed is actually right next to the window, and since I sleep during the day, I had to put up these blackout curtains. They're supposed to block out 100% of the light, but there's always this tiny crack at the bottom where the sun sneaks in. It drives me crazy. I tried taping it down with duct tape once, but it peeled the paint off the windowsill, and my landlord got so mad. He's this older guy who always smells like peppermint and cigars, which is such a weird combination. He actually threatened to keep my security deposit over it, which I really need if I'm ever going to move out of that place. The plumbing is terrible anyway. But, um... yeah, I don't really count how many times I write in the journal. Just when the feeling hits me, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It always feels... out of my hands, I guess. Once I start eating, it's like I keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically. Even while I'm sitting there thinking, 'Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,' I just don't actually stop until the food is gone or I'm too uncomfortable to keep going. I've been trying to get back into sketching lately. Mostly charcoal stuff, portraits and things. I used to do it all the time in high school, but then charcoal is just so messy, you know? It gets all over your hands and under your fingernails. I spend half my shift scrubbing floors and wiping down counters, so the last thing I want is to look like my hands are covered in soot. I actually found this special pumice soap that mechanics use? It smells exactly like oranges, which reminds me of this orchard my parents took me to when I was little. We picked so many oranges we ended up having to make marmalade, and I ruined my favorite yellow shirt because the jam got everywhere. But anyway, yeah, I don't ever really feel in control when I'm eating like that. I just can't stop once I start.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don't own a scale or anything, but my work pants seem to fit about the same as they always do. They're these terrible, stiff navy blue cargo pants they make us wear. I swear the material is completely indestructible, but it's so uncomfortable. I've washed them a million times trying to soften them up, but the washing machines in my apartment building are basically useless. They always smell like mildew. I usually end up dragging all my stuff to the laundromat two blocks over. Last time I was there, someone left a single red sock hidden in the dryer, and it turned all my white undershirts this weird dusty pink color. I tried soaking them in bleach, but honestly, I smell enough bleach at work. I spend half my shift scrubbing the bathrooms on the third floor of this office building, and the smell just clings to your hair. There's this one woman on that floor who leaves half-drank lattes on her desk every Friday, and by Monday they're practically a science experiment. I don't understand how people can just leave a mess like that for someone else to deal with. But anyway, yeah, to answer your question, my weight hasn't really changed that I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. That's actually... that's kind of why I came in to see you. After it happens, I just feel so gross and disappointed in myself. It's like, I'm 24, I should have more control over myself, you know? And then I end up writing all those awful things about myself in my journal. The journal is this leather-bound one my sister gave me for my birthday last year. She got it from this little artisan market downtown. They always have these incredible food trucks there. There's this one that does Korean BBQ tacos, and the line is always wrapped around the block. I tried making my own at home once, but I couldn't get the marinade right. It calls for Asian pears, which are surprisingly hard to find at my regular grocery store. I usually have to take the bus all the way to the international market on the other side of town, and the buses here are just so unreliable. Last week, I waited forty-five minutes in the freezing rain because two buses in a row just drove right past my stop without even slowing down. By the time I got to work, my shoes were completely soaked through, and trying to mop floors in squeaky, wet sneakers is the worst. But, um... yeah, I feel really guilty and just... bad about myself after I eat like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I eat really fast during those times. I'll just be standing at the kitchen counter, or sometimes I'm sitting on my bed with my sketchbook still open, and I'm just... I'm barely even tasting anything, honestly. It's like I'm just trying to get it down before I can think too much about what I'm doing. It reminds me of how fast I have to eat on my lunch breaks at work. They only give us thirty minutes, and by the time I get all my cleaning supplies locked up in the utility closet—which the lock always gets stuck on, by the way. I've told the building manager, Mr. Henderson, like five times that it needs WD-40, but he never listens. He's always too busy talking on his cell phone in the lobby. He has this really loud, booming voice, and you can hear him echoing off the marble floors from all the way down the hall. Anyway, by the time I get my stuff put away and wash my hands, I have like fifteen minutes left to actually eat. So I usually just inhale a sandwich or whatever I brought. I've been trying to bring healthier things, like those little pre-packaged salads? But the lettuce always gets kind of slimy by the end of the week. But yeah, when I have those episodes at home, it's definitely like that. Just really, really fast.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the amount of food you eat during these episodes is more than what most people would eat in the same amount of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's way more than a normal person would eat. Like, on some nights after my cleaning shift, I'll stop at the convenience store on the corner—the one with the flickering neon sign that drives me crazy. The owner, this guy named Sal, he never fixes it. He's always sitting behind the counter watching these really old black-and-white westerns on a tiny portable TV. I don't know how he can even see the screen, it's so small and grainy. But anyway, I'll stop there just meaning to grab dinner, and I end up buying a big bowl of ramen, several rice balls, some fried chicken, and a bunch of sweets. And then I take it all back to my room and eat the whole thing in like an hour or two. My room is so small, too, so the smell of the fried chicken just lingers for days. I tried lighting one of those expensive soy candles my mom got me for Christmas to cover it up. It's supposed to smell like \"ocean breeze\" or something, but it just smells like cheap soap. My mom is obsessed with candles. She has this whole closet in her house just dedicated to storing them, all organized by season. She gets really upset if you burn a winter candle in the summer. But um, yeah, it's a lot of food. Way more than I should be eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've ever had one of these eating episodes without feeling guilty or bad about yourself afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, never. That's... I think I mentioned this earlier, but that's the whole reason I'm here. After it happens, every single time, I just feel so incredibly gross and disappointed in myself. It's like, I'm 24 years old, I should have more control over myself, you know? And then I end up writing all those awful things about myself in my journal. The leather one my sister got me. She's always been the one with her life together, honestly. She just got promoted to regional manager at her company, and she gets to travel all over the place. Last month she went to Chicago and brought me back this deep-dish pizza. It was frozen, obviously, packed in dry ice, but it was still so good. The dry ice was actually really cool, I put a piece of it in a bowl of warm water in the sink and watched it fog up the whole kitchen. It looked like a haunted house or something. I love Halloween. It's the only time of year I actually like going out, because you can dress up and just be someone else for a night. Last year I went as a ghost, but like, a really fancy Victorian ghost. I found this amazing lace dress at a thrift store... but anyway, no, I've never had an episode where I didn't feel just completely terrible and guilty afterwards. It always ends with me feeling bad and writing in that journal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode of eating a large amount of food in a short time without feeling guilty or bad about yourself afterwards?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? Or... wait, did you ask it differently? I'm pretty sure I just said that, right? That I always feel terrible afterwards. I mean, I don't think I've ever *not* felt guilty. It's like this heavy feeling in my stomach, which is funny because my stomach is already full, but it's a different kind of heavy. Kind of like when I accidentally dropped my supervisor's master key ring down the elevator shaft last month. Oh my gosh, that was a nightmare. The keys made this horrible clanging sound all the way down, just echoing. We had to call the elevator maintenance guy, Frank. He's this older guy who always wears suspenders and smells like peppermint and stale coffee. He was so mad, grumbling the whole time he was fishing them out of the pit. He kept talking about how back in his day, people respected their tools. I tried to offer him one of my peppermint candies to smooth things over, but he just glared at me. I get those candies in bulk from this warehouse store my sister has a membership to. You can buy like a five-pound bag for ten dollars. But yeah, no, I've never had an episode without feeling guilty. I thought I just answered that, but maybe I got distracted. Sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have these eating episodes and the feelings that follow them caused problems for you at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I still make it to my cleaning shifts, and I do these freelance design events sometimes, so I'm getting by. But the next day at work, I just feel so distracted and ashamed. It's like this cloud hanging over me. And sometimes I put off my sketching because I don't want to open my journal and look at the awful things I wrote about myself the night before. I use the same notebook for my sketches and my writing, which seemed like a good idea at the time because I like keeping everything in one place. It's this really nice leather one, like I said, and the paper is that thick, creamy kind that doesn't bleed through when you use ink. I love using these Japanese brush pens for my designs. They have such a fluid stroke. I actually ordered a new set online last week because my black one was drying out, but the delivery driver left the package on the wrong porch. He left it at apartment 4B instead of 4D. The lady in 4B is this older woman who has like, six cats, and the hallway always smells like pine litter. She's really sweet, though, she brought the package over and even gave me a slice of zucchini bread she made. I'm not a huge fan of zucchini in baked goods, it feels like it shouldn't be there, you know? Like carrots in cake. My sister makes an amazing carrot cake, though, with real cream cheese frosting. She uses this special vanilla extract she got in Mexico... but anyway, yeah, it definitely causes some problems because I just feel so out of it and avoid my drawing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having these eating episodes for more than three months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely more than three months. For the past several months, it's been like... well, usually at least one night every single week where I get off work, go buy way too much food, and just eat it all in one stretch. I usually stop at this 24-hour grocery store on my way home from the office building I clean. It's so unbelievably bright in there at 2 AM, the fluorescent lights always give me a bit of a headache. They have this one night cashier, I think his name is Toby? He always wears these really loud Hawaiian shirts, even when it's freezing outside. I asked him about it once, and he said he collects them. He has like, over fifty of them. I can't imagine having that many shirts, honestly, my closet is so tiny. I live in this older apartment building and the closets are basically just glorified cupboards. I actually had to buy one of those rolling clothing racks just to hang up my jackets. The wheels on it are kind of squeaky, though, so I try not to move it around too much so I don't bother the neighbors below me. But anyway, um... right, the timeline. Yeah, I can't really remember a recent month where it stopped for more than a week or so. It's just been a regular thing every week for a while now.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat during these episodes in secret, or do you eat with other people around?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I never eat with anyone else around when it happens. It's always just me, by myself. I make sure I'm alone. I usually wait until my roommate is asleep or out. She works these really weird hours at a call center, so sometimes she's gone all night. She handles customer service for some big electronics company, and she's always telling me these crazy stories about people yelling at her over broken toasters and stuff. I don't know how she does it. I'd cry if someone yelled at me on the phone like that. I actually hate talking on the phone anyway. Even making a doctor's appointment gives me anxiety, I always prefer to book things online if I can. But yeah, no, I definitely hide it. I wouldn't want anyone to see me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, that's exactly how it feels. It's like... once I start, I just keep reaching for the next thing, almost automatically. Even while I'm sitting there thinking, \"Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,\" I don't actually stop. I just keep going until all the food is gone or I feel too sick to my stomach. It's like my hands are just moving on their own. I actually sprained my wrist a few years ago, not from eating, obviously, but from falling off my bike. I used to ride this vintage Schwinn cruiser everywhere. It was this beautiful seafoam green color. I found it at a garage sale for like, twenty bucks, which was such a steal because the tires were still in really good shape. The guy selling it was moving to Florida and just trying to get rid of everything. He had this whole collection of old vinyl records, too, mostly jazz stuff. I'm not really into jazz, it feels a little too chaotic for me. I prefer music with a really steady beat, like synth-pop. I've been listening to this one band from Sweden a lot lately, their lead singer has this amazing, ethereal voice. I tried to buy tickets to see them when they came to town last month, but they sold out in like, five minutes. The ticketing website crashed and everything, it was so frustrating. I ended up just staying home and watching a documentary about deep-sea creatures instead. Did you know there are jellyfish that can basically live forever? They just revert back to their polyp stage when they get old or injured. It's wild. But um, anyway, yeah, I just can't seem to stop myself once I start eating, no matter how much I tell myself to.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you lose interest in things you normally enjoy during these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um, lose interest? I'm not really sure if I lose interest, honestly. I don't think I've ever really noticed that happening. I mean, usually when I'm eating like that, I'm just so focused on the food that I'm not really thinking about doing anything else anyway. It's not like I'm trying to draw or watch a movie at the same time. I tried watching a movie once while I was eating, it was that new sci-fi one with the time loops? My roommate insisted we watch it, but I couldn't really follow the plot. Time travel stuff always confuses me. Like, if you go back and change something, wouldn't that mean you never had a reason to go back in the first place? It just makes my head spin. I prefer movies that are more straightforward, like romantic comedies or documentaries. Oh, I watched this amazing documentary about competitive dog grooming last week. Have you seen it? They dye the poodles to look like pandas and tigers and stuff. It's so strange, but the dogs seem to really enjoy all the attention. I grew up with a golden retriever named Buster, and he hated baths. He would always try to hide under the porch whenever he saw the shampoo bottle. We had this big wrap-around porch on the house I grew up in, it was painted this really bright yellow color. My mom picked it out. She loved bright colors, she always wore these neon pink gardening gloves. She had this huge tomato garden in the backyard, and we'd eat fresh tomatoes all summer long. But, um... what were you asking? Oh, right, losing interest. Yeah, I don't think that's really a thing for me during those times. I just haven't noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these eating episodes regularly over the past year?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, regularly... I'd say it's been going on for about a year and two months now. It started right around the time I began trying to switch from my cleaning job into design, but I had to keep all my old janitorial shifts to pay the rent. It's just a lot, you know? Mopping floors at night and then trying to build a portfolio during the day. I actually work at this big corporate office building downtown, and the floors in the main lobby are this really porous travertine marble. It looks beautiful, but it's an absolute nightmare to keep clean. If someone spills coffee and you don't get to it in like five minutes, it stains. We have to use this special pH-neutral cleaner that smells like faint lemons. I actually don't mind the smell of lemons, it reminds me of this lemonade stand I had when I was a kid. My dad built the stand for me out of some scrap wood from a deck he was tearing down. He painted it white, but the paint started peeling almost immediately because he didn't use a primer. He was always rushing through projects like that. He tried to fix the plumbing under the kitchen sink once and ended up flooding the entire first floor. We had to live in a hotel for a week while they ripped up the hardwood. The hotel had a pool, though, which was pretty cool for a ten-year-old. I used to practice holding my breath underwater for as long as I could. I think my record was like, forty-five seconds. But um, yeah, so it's been about fourteen months since all this eating stuff started.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I'm not really sure if I lose interest, honestly. I don't think I've ever really noticed that happening. My roommate insisted we watch it, but I couldn't really follow the plot. Time travel stuff always confuses me. Like, if you go back and change something, wouldn't that mean you never had a reason to go back in the first place? It just makes my head spin. They dye the poodles to look like pandas and tigers and stuff. It's so strange, but the dogs seem to really enjoy all the attention. I grew up with a golden retriever named Buster, and he hated baths. He would always try to hide under the porch whenever he saw the shampoo bottle. We had this big wrap-around porch on the house I grew up in, it was painted this really bright yellow color. My mom picked it out. She loved bright colors, she always wore these neon pink gardening gloves. She had this huge tomato garden in the backyard, and we'd eat fresh tomatoes all summer long. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, stressed or upset? I don't really know if I can connect it to a specific feeling like that. I've never really thought about it. Sometimes it happens when I'm just sitting around, not really feeling anything in particular. Speaking of sitting around, I bought this new ergonomic chair for my desk at home, because my back was killing me from hunching over my sketchbook. It's one of those ones with the mesh back so you don't get all sweaty. I ordered it online, and the box it came in was massive. I had to drag it up three flights of stairs to my apartment. My neighbor, Mrs. Higgins, saw me struggling and offered to help, but she's like eighty years old and uses a cane, so I obviously told her no. She's really sweet, though. She always bakes these little oatmeal raisin cookies and leaves them outside my door. I actually hate raisins. I don't understand why people ruin perfectly good cookies by putting dried-up grapes in them. It's like a trick. You think it's a chocolate chip, and then you bite into it and it's just squishy disappointment. I used to pick them out when I was a kid and hide them under the edge of my plate. My grandmother would always catch me and tell me I was wasting food. She grew up during the Depression, so she was super strict about finishing everything on your plate. She kept a coffee can full of bacon grease on her stove and used it to cook everything. But um, anyway, yeah, I don't really notice if I'm anxious or stressed when I start eating. I just haven't paid attention to that.

I usually end up dragging all my stuff to the laundromat two blocks over. Last time I was there, someone left a single red sock hidden in the dryer, and it turned all my white undershirts this weird dusty pink color. I tried soaking them in bleach, but honestly, I smell enough bleach at work. I spend half my shift scrubbing the bathrooms on the third floor of this office building, and the smell just clings to your hair. There's this one woman on that floor who leaves half-drank lattes on her desk every Friday, and by Monday they're practically a science experiment. I don't understand how people can just leave a mess like that for someone else to deal with. I mean, I still make it to my cleaning shifts, and I do these freelance design events sometimes, so I'm getting by. But the next day at work, I just feel so distracted and ashamed. It's like this cloud hanging over me. And sometimes I put off my sketching because I don't want to open my journal and look at the awful things I wrote about myself the night before. I use the same notebook for my sketches and my writing, which seemed like a good idea at the time because I like keeping everything in one place. It's this really nice leather one, like I said, and the paper is that thick, creamy kind that doesn't bleed through when you use ink. I love using these Japanese brush pens for my designs. They have such a fluid stroke. I actually ordered a new set online last week because my black one was drying out, but the delivery driver left the package on the wrong porch. He left it at apartment 4B instead of 4D. The lady in 4B is this older woman who has like, six cats, and the hallway always smells like pine litter. She's really sweet, though, she brought the package over and even gave me a slice of zucchini bread she made. I'm not a huge fan of zucchini in baked goods, it feels like it shouldn't be there, you know? Like carrots in cake. My sister makes an amazing carrot cake, though, with real cream cheese frosting. She uses this special vanilla extract she got in Mexico... but anyway, yeah, it definitely causes some problems because I just feel so out of it and avoid my drawing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having these eating episodes for more than three months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely more than three months. For the past several months, it's been like... well, usually at least one night every single week where I get off work, go buy way too much food, and just eat it all in one stretch. I usually stop at this 24-hour grocery store on my way home from the office building I clean. It's so unbelievably bright in there at 2 AM, the fluorescent lights always give me a bit of a headache. They have this one night cashier, I think his name is Toby? He always wears these really loud Hawaiian shirts, even when it's freezing outside. I asked him about it once, and he said he collects them. He has like, over fifty of them. I can't imagine having that many shirts, honestly, my closet is so tiny. I live in this older apartment building and the closets are basically just glorified cupboards. I actually had to buy one of those rolling clothing racks just to hang up my jackets. The wheels on it are kind of squeaky, though, so I try not to move it around too much so I don't bother the neighbors below me. But anyway, um... right, the timeline. Yeah, I can't really remember a recent month where it stopped for more than a week or so. It's just been a regular thing every week for a while now.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat during these episodes in secret, or do you eat with other people around?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I never eat with anyone else around when it happens. It's always just me, by myself. I make sure I'm alone. I usually wait until my roommate is asleep or out. She works these really weird hours at a call center, so sometimes she's gone all night. She handles customer service for some big electronics company, and she's always telling me these crazy stories about people yelling at her over broken toasters and stuff. I don't know how she does it. I'd cry if someone yelled at me on the phone like that. I actually hate talking on the phone anyway. Even making a doctor's appointment gives me anxiety, I always prefer to book things online if I can. But yeah, no, I definitely hide it. I wouldn't want anyone to see me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, that's exactly how it feels. It's like... once I start, I just keep reaching for the next thing, almost automatically. Even while I'm sitting there thinking, \"Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,\" I don't actually stop. I just keep going until all the food is gone or I feel too sick to my stomach. It's like my hands are just moving on their own. I actually sprained my wrist a few years ago, not from eating, obviously, but from falling off my bike. I used to ride this vintage Schwinn cruiser everywhere. It was this beautiful seafoam green color. I found it at a garage sale for like, twenty bucks, which was such a steal because the tires were still in really good shape. The guy selling it was moving to Florida and just trying to get rid of everything. He had this whole collection of old vinyl records, too, mostly jazz stuff. I'm not really into jazz, it feels a little too chaotic for me. I prefer music with a really steady beat, like synth-pop. I've been listening to this one band from Sweden a lot lately, their lead singer has this amazing, ethereal voice. I tried to buy tickets to see them when they came to town last month, but they sold out in like, five minutes. The ticketing website crashed and everything, it was so frustrating. I ended up just staying home and watching a documentary about deep-sea creatures instead. Did you know there are jellyfish that can basically live forever? They just revert back to their polyp stage when they get old or injured. It's wild. But um, anyway, yeah, I just can't seem to stop myself once I start eating, no matter how much I tell myself to.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you lose interest in things you normally enjoy during these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um, lose interest? I'm not really sure if I lose interest, honestly. I don't think I've ever really noticed that happening. I mean, usually when I'm eating like that, I'm just so focused on the food that I'm not really thinking about doing anything else anyway. It's not like I'm trying to draw or watch a movie at the same time. I tried watching a movie once while I was eating, it was that new sci-fi one with the time loops? My roommate insisted we watch it, but I couldn't really follow the plot. Time travel stuff always confuses me. Like, if you go back and change something, wouldn't that mean you never had a reason to go back in the first place? It just makes my head spin. I prefer movies that are more straightforward, like romantic comedies or documentaries. Oh, I watched this amazing documentary about competitive dog grooming last week. Have you seen it? They dye the poodles to look like pandas and tigers and stuff. It's so strange, but the dogs seem to really enjoy all the attention. I grew up with a golden retriever named Buster, and he hated baths. He would always try to hide under the porch whenever he saw the shampoo bottle. We had this big wrap-around porch on the house I grew up in, it was painted this really bright yellow color. My mom picked it out. She loved bright colors, she always wore these neon pink gardening gloves. She had this huge tomato garden in the backyard, and we'd eat fresh tomatoes all summer long. But, um... what were you asking? Oh, right, losing interest. Yeah, I don't think that's really a thing for me during those times. I just haven't noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these eating episodes regularly over the past year?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, regularly... I'd say it's been going on for about a year and two months now. It started right around the time I began trying to switch from my cleaning job into design, but I had to keep all my old janitorial shifts to pay the rent. It's just a lot, you know? Mopping floors at night and then trying to build a portfolio during the day. I actually work at this big corporate office building downtown, and the floors in the main lobby are this really porous travertine marble. It looks beautiful, but it's an absolute nightmare to keep clean. If someone spills coffee and you don't get to it in like five minutes, it stains. We have to use this special pH-neutral cleaner that smells like faint lemons. I actually don't mind the smell of lemons, it reminds me of this lemonade stand I had when I was a kid. My dad built the stand for me out of some scrap wood from a deck he was tearing down. He painted it white, but the paint started peeling almost immediately because he didn't use a primer. He was always rushing through projects like that. He tried to fix the plumbing under the kitchen sink once and ended up flooding the entire first floor. We had to live in a hotel for a week while they ripped up the hardwood. The hotel had a pool, though, which was pretty cool for a ten-year-old. I used to practice holding my breath underwater for as long as I could. I think my record was like, forty-five seconds. But um, yeah, so it's been about fourteen months since all this eating stuff started.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat like this when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: Um, stressed or upset? I don't really know if I can connect it to a specific feeling like that. I've never really thought about it. Sometimes it happens when I'm just sitting around, not really feeling anything in particular. Speaking of sitting around, I bought this new ergonomic chair for my desk at home, because my back was killing me from hunching over my sketchbook. It's one of those ones with the mesh back so you don't get all sweaty. I ordered it online, and the box it came in was massive. I had to drag it up three flights of stairs to my apartment. My neighbor, Mrs. Higgins, saw me struggling and offered to help, but she's like eighty years old and uses a cane, so I obviously told her no. She's really sweet, though. She always bakes these little oatmeal raisin cookies and leaves them outside my door. I actually hate raisins. I don't understand why people ruin perfectly good cookies by putting dried-up grapes in them. It's like a trick. You think it's a chocolate chip, and then you bite into it and it's just squishy disappointment. I used to pick them out when I was a kid and hide them under the edge of my plate. My grandmother would always catch me and tell me I was wasting food. She grew up during the Depression, so she was super strict about finishing everything on your plate. She kept a coffee can full of bacon grease on her stove and used it to cook everything. But um, anyway, yeah, I don't really notice if I'm anxious or stressed when I start eating. I just haven't paid attention to that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I could have used it as a doorstop. My apartment building has these really heavy fire doors in the hallways, and they slam shut so loudly. Whenever someone moves in or out, they have to prop them open with whatever they can find. The last guy who moved out left this ugly ceramic frog propping the door open. It was painted neon green with these creepy yellow eyes. I don't know why anyone would buy something like that. I prefer more minimalist decor, you know? Just clean lines and neutral colors. It makes a space feel so much bigger. My apartment is tiny, so I need all the help I can get. But, yeah, to answer your question... I don't really know if I'm emotional before I eat. I just haven't made that connection."
  }
